17	function sectionTitle(unit: ConditionalUnit, index: number): string {
18	  if (unit.id === 'orientation') return ['What a conditional says', 'A rule needs a case fact', 'Keep the strength of the claim'][index] || 'Read the complete guarantee';
19	  if (unit.id === 'rules-facts') return ['Separate the fact and rule', 'What each fact can establish', 'A chain produces a new rule', 'Show the match and explain why', 'Four facts, one unchanged rule', 'A requirement is not a completed action'][index] || 'Check the whole claim';
20	  if (unit.area === 'translation') return ['Read the indicator in context', 'Preserve the complete ideas', 'Check the meaning before shortening', 'Use a meaning check', 'Practice the translation', 'Recognize related expressions', 'Keep the direction precise'][index] || 'Compare the wording';
21	  if (unit.area === 'linking') return ['Find the exact connection', 'Keep rules separate from facts', 'Preserve subjects and scope', 'Expose a hidden connection', 'Make the path visible', 'Check intermediate conclusions', 'Match the complete requirement'][index] || 'Test the proposed connection';
22	  return index === 0 ? 'Understand the relationship' : index === 1 ? 'Compare the possible cases' : index === 2 ? 'Keep the whole claim' : `Check the reasoning ${index - 1}`;
23	}
24	
25	export function ConditionalLessonNote({ unit, onComplete, continueHref, continueLabel, previousHref, reviewed }: ConditionalLessonNoteProps) {
26	  if (hasOpeningLesson(unit.id)) return <ConditionalOpeningLesson key={unit.id} unit={unit} onComplete={onComplete} continueHref={continueHref} continueLabel={continueLabel} previousHref={previousHref} reviewed={reviewed} />;
27	  return <div className="conditional-note">
28	    <DynamicIslandTOC key={unit.id} selector=".conditional-note [data-toc]" />
29	    <header className="cn-hero"><div><p className="cn-label">Conditional logic</p><h1>{unit.title}</h1><p>{unit.goal}</p></div><a className="cn-start" href="#conditional-note-start">Read the lesson<span aria-hidden="true">↓</span></a></header>
30	    <article id="conditional-note-start" className="cn-paper">
31	      {unit.id === 'orientation' && <section data-toc data-toc-title="A promise about qualifying cases" className="cn-section"><p className="cn-label">Start with the meaning</p><h2>A promise about qualifying cases</h2><p>A conditional connects two complete ideas. It says: if the starting condition is met, the required result must follow. It does not say that the starting condition has already happened.</p><div className="cn-diagram"><span>If this condition holds</span><span aria-hidden="true">→</span><span>This result must follow</span></div><p>The left side is the trigger. The right side is what the rule guarantees. Understand that promise before memorizing indicator words.</p></section>}
32	      {unit.teaching.map((paragraph, index) => <section key={index} className="cn-section" data-toc data-toc-title={sectionTitle(unit, index)}><p className="cn-label">{String(index + 1).padStart(2, '0')}</p><h2>{sectionTitle(unit, index)}</h2><p>{paragraph}</p></section>)}
33	      <section className="cn-section" data-toc data-toc-title="Work through an example"><h2>Work through an example</h2><div className="cn-example">{unit.workedExample.statements.map((statement, index) => <p key={index}>{statement}</p>)}</div><ol className="cn-reasoning">{unit.workedExample.reasoning.map((reason, index) => <li key={index}>{reason}</li>)}</ol><div className="cn-result"><p className="cn-label">What follows</p><p><strong>{unit.workedExample.conclusion}</strong></p></div></section>
34	      <section className="cn-section" data-toc data-toc-title="Try the relationship"><h2>Try the relationship</h2><LessonInteraction unit={unit} /></section>
35	      <section className="cn-section" data-toc data-toc-title="A method to check your work"><h2>A method to check your work</h2><ol className="cn-method">{unit.method.map((step, index) => <li key={index}>{step}</li>)}</ol></section>
36	      <section id="repair" className="cn-section" data-toc data-toc-title="Catch the tempting mistake"><h2>Catch the tempting mistake</h2><div className="cn-contrast"><div><p className="cn-label">The tempting move</p><p>{unit.commonMistake.mistake}</p></div><div><p className="cn-label">Check it this way</p><p>{unit.commonMistake.repair}</p></div></div></section>
37	      <footer className="cn-footer"><p>{reviewed ? 'You marked this lesson as read. You can revisit it whenever you need.' : 'Marking this lesson as read records review, not mastery.'}</p><div className="cn-controls">{previousHref && <Link to={previousHref}>Previous lesson</Link>}<button type="button" onClick={onComplete}>{reviewed ? 'Lesson read' : 'Mark lesson as read'}</button><Link className="cn-primary" to={continueHref}>Continue to practice →</Link></div></footer>
38	    </article>
39	  </div>;
40	}

410	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Premises And Conclusions" className="space-y-8">
411	          <SectionHeading title="Arguments Are Premises and Conclusions" subtitle="The core structure" />
412	          <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.84)]">
413	            Arguments are quite simple at their core. An argument is made up of two things: premises and conclusions.
414	          </p>
415	          <QuoteCard className="bg-[#FBF8F1] pr-6">
416	            <div className="space-y-5">
417	              <div>
418	                <p className="font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-[#1a1a1a]">Premises support conclusions.</p>
419	                <p>Premises are the evidence.</p>
420	              </div>
421	              <div>
422	                <p className="font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-[#1a1a1a]">Conclusions rely on premises.</p>
423	                <p>Conclusions are the author's claim.</p>
424	              </div>
425	            </div>
426	          </QuoteCard>
427	          <p className="text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
428	            <strong>That's it.</strong> Arguments are just premises and conclusions. They have a lot of complicated
429	            baggage we'll introduce soon, but at their core, arguments are always this simple.
430	          </p>
431	        </LessonSection>
432	
433	        <LessonSection tocTitle="A conclusion needs support" className="space-y-8">
434	          <SupportSimulator />
435	        </LessonSection>
436	
437	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Core Takeaway" className="space-y-8 border-b-0">
438	          <SectionHeading title="Core Takeaway" subtitle="Evidence and claims" />
439	          <QuoteCard className="bg-[#FBF8F1] pr-6">
440	            <p>
441	              <strong>Arguments are the relationships between Premises and Conclusions.</strong>
442	            </p>
443	            <p>Premises hold the conclusion up. Without premises a conclusion has no support.</p>
444	          </QuoteCard>
445	        </LessonSection>
446	      </>
447	    ),
448	  },
449	  premises: {
450	    eyebrow: "",
451	    title: "Premises",
452	    subtitle: "Premises are the facts and evidence you accept as true; the real question is how they connect to the conclusion.",
453	    content: (
454	      <>
455	        <div className="space-y-8">
456	          <PremiseSystemFrame>
457	            <PremiseSystemSection tocTitle="Statements Of Fact" className="space-y-9 bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,rgba(255,255,255,0.88)_0%,rgba(255,252,245,0.72)_100%)]">
458	              <SectionHeading title="Premises" subtitle="Statements of fact" />
459	              
460	              <QuoteCard className="bg-[#FBF8F1] pr-6">
461	                <p className="font-semibold text-lg text-[#1a1a1a]">Premises are just the facts. The evidence.</p>
462	                <p className="text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">They describe how the world is, and on the LSAT, you accept them as true, full stop.</p>
463	              </QuoteCard>
464	
465	              <div className="space-y-6 text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
466	                <p className="font-medium text-[#1a1a1a]">In real life, we attack the facts:</p>
467	                <div className="border-l-4 border-[#E8D08B] pl-6 py-2 space-y-3">
468	                    <p className="italic text-gray-700">"We should cancel the picnic because the park is flooded."</p>
469	                    <p className="font-semibold text-[#1a1a1a]">"Actually, the park is dry and the forecast is clear."</p>
470	                </div>
471	                <p>In real life, you'd attack the facts they used to support the conclusion.</p>
472	                <div className="relative overflow-hidden rounded-xl border-l-4 border-[#B91C1C] bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,rgba(254,242,242,0.95)_0%,rgba(254,226,226,0.75)_100%)] px-6 py-5 space-y-2">
473	                  <p className="text-[11px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-[#B91C1C]">Never on the LSAT</p>
474	                  <p className="text-[17px] font-semibold text-[#1a1a1a]">Do not attack the premises.</p>
475	                  <p className="text-[16px] leading-[1.65] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
476	                    The LSAT will <strong>never</strong> ask you to question whether a premise is true. Premises are handed
477	                    to you as fact — arguing "actually, the park isn't flooded" is not a move that exists in this test.
478	                    If you catch yourself doubting the evidence, stop. That instinct is for real life, not for LR.
479	                  </p>
480	                </div>
481	              </div>
482	
483	              
484	              <PremiseRememberCallout>
485	                <p>On the LSAT, premises are given. Accept them as true, then ask what they are being used to support.</p>
486	              </PremiseRememberCallout>
487	              <PremiseKeyRule>
488	                <p>
489	                  What you focus on instead is how the premises connect to the conclusion. Whether they actually support the
490	                  claim the author is making. That's the whole game.
491	                </p>
492	              </PremiseKeyRule>
493	            </PremiseSystemSection>
494	
495	            <PremiseSystemSection tocTitle="What Premises Are And Aren't" className="space-y-9">
496	              <SectionHeading title="What Premises Are and Aren't" subtitle="What they do in the argument" />
497	              <PremiseSummarySlabs />
498	              <div className="space-y-4 text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
499	                <p>Premises do the argument's heavy lifting. They're the evidence that makes the conclusion worth anything.</p>
500	                <p>
501	                  They are not questionable. Not on the LSAT. They don't depend on the conclusion or on each other. They just
502	                  exist, solid, already true, handed to you for free.
503	                </p>
504	              </div>
505	              <div className="space-y-4 text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
506	                <p>
507	                  It doesn't ask you to poke holes in the evidence. It asks you whether the evidence actually leads where the
508	                  author thinks it does. That's a harder, more useful question, and it's the one that gets you points.
509	                </p>
510	                <p>
511	                  <strong>Premises are defined by their relationship to the conclusion.</strong> That's the key. A sentence isn't a premise because of what it says. It's a premise because of what it's doing in the argument: supporting the claim.
512	                </p>
513	              </div>
514	            </PremiseSystemSection>
515	
516	            <PremiseSystemSection tocTitle="Premise Indicators" className="space-y-9 bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,rgba(255,255,255,0.55)_0%,rgba(255,250,241,0.78)_100%)]" border={false}>
517	              <SectionHeading title="Premise Indicators" subtitle="FABS" />
518	              <div className="space-y-4 text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
519	                <p>Certain words signal that a premise is coming. Learn them and you'll start spotting argument structure automatically.</p>
520	                <p>The four big ones: <strong>For, After all, Because, Since.</strong> FABS.</p>
521	                <p>
522	                  When you see any of these, a premise is right behind it. The author is handing you evidence. Take it,
523	                  accept it, and ask what it's being used to support.
524	                </p>
525	                <p>
526	                  <strong>FABS. Memorize it.</strong>
527	                </p>
528	              </div>
529	              <div className="space-y-6">
530	                <PremiseIndicatorLab />
531	                <PremiseSupportCheck />
532	              </div>
533	              <PremiseRememberCallout>
534	                <p>
535	                  Accept premises as true. Don't debate them. Focus on whether they support the conclusion.
536	                </p>
537	              </PremiseRememberCallout>
538	            </PremiseSystemSection>
539	          </PremiseSystemFrame>
540	        </div>
541	      </>
542	    ),
543	  },
544	  conclusions: {
545	    eyebrow: "THE MAIN POINT",
546	    title: "Conclusions: The Author's Claim",
547	    subtitle: "Conclusions are the author's claims and judgments; they are the part of the argument you question.",
548	    content: (
549	      <>
550	        <LessonSection tocTitle="The Author's Claim" className="space-y-8">
551	          <div className="space-y-4 text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
552	            <p>Conclusions are the judgments the author makes. They are the main point the author wants you to accept. This is the part of the argument you should examine most carefully.</p>
553	          </div>
554	
555	          <QuoteCard className="bg-[#FBF8F1] pr-6 border-l-4 border-l-[#D5AD5F]">
556	            <p>
557	              <strong>Key rule:</strong> The conclusion is the part of the argument you should be most suspicious of. Ask whether it is too strong, whether it goes beyond the evidence, whether the author assumed something missing, and whether the premises really get us there.
558	            </p>
559	          </QuoteCard>
560	
561	          <div className="grid gap-6 md:grid-cols-2">
562	            <div className="rounded-[12px] border border-[#D5E7CF] bg-[#FBFDF8] p-5">
563	              <span className="text-[12px] font-bold text-[#2F5B35] uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">ARE:</span>
564	              <ul className="list-disc list-inside text-[16px] text-[#2F5B35] space-y-1">
565	                <li>Author's claim</li>
566	                <li>The point argued for</li>
567	                <li>Part you question</li>
568	              </ul>
569	            </div>
570	            <div className="rounded-[12px] border border-[#E6D6B2] bg-[#FEFBF2] p-5">
571	              <span className="text-[12px] font-bold text-[#74541B] uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">ARE NOT:</span>
572	              <ul className="list-disc list-inside text-[16px] text-[#74541B] space-y-1">
573	                <li>Automatically proven</li>
574	                <li>Self-supporting</li>
575	                <li>Always clear</li>
576	              </ul>
577	            </div>
578	          </div>
579	        </LessonSection>
580	
581	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Conclusion Indicators" className="space-y-8">
582	          <ConclusionIndicatorLab />
583	          <IndicatorPopQuiz />
584	          <InteractiveCard prominent className="bg-[#FFFDFC] p-6 md:p-8">
585	            <div className="space-y-5">
586	              <div className="space-y-2">
587	                <div className="text-[11px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.48)]">
588	                  One quick rep
589	                </div>
590	                <h3 className="font-serif text-[30px] italic leading-tight text-[#1a1a1a]">
591	                  Pick the conclusion before the page tells you
592	                </h3>
593	                <p className="text-[17px] leading-[1.65] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.76)]">
594	                  Read the whole argument first. Then pick the sentence the rest is trying to support.
595	                </p>
596	              </div>
597	              <ConclusionPicker
598	                stimulus="The city museum has operated at a deficit for three years. However, the museum provides free educational programs for local schools. Therefore, the city should continue funding it."
599	                candidates={[
600	                  {
601	                    id: "museum-background",
602	                    text: "The city museum has operated at a deficit for three years.",
603	                    is_main: false,
604	                    rationale: "This sets the scene. It matters to the topic, but the author is not trying to prove that this deficit exists.",
605	                  },
606	                  {
607	                    id: "museum-premise",
608	                    text: "The museum provides free educational programs for local schools.",
609	                    is_main: false,
610	                    rationale: "This is support. It is one reason the author gives for the funding recommendation.",
611	                  },
612	                  {
613	                    id: "museum-conclusion",
614	                    text: "The city should continue funding it.",
615	                    is_main: true,
616	                    rationale: "This is the author's recommendation, and the educational-programs sentence is offered to support it.",
617	                  },
618	                ]}
619	              />
620	            </div>
621	          </InteractiveCard>
622	        </LessonSection>
623	
624	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Do Not Just Hunt" className="space-y-8 border-b-0">
625	          <SectionHeading title="Don't Just Hunt for Indicator Words" subtitle="Read structure, not just packaging" />
626	          
627	          <div className="space-y-6 text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
628	            <p>Here's the thing, though. Indicator words are a tool, not a crutch.</p>
629	            
630	            <QuoteCard className="bg-[#FBF8F1] pr-6">
631	              <p>
632	                <strong>The goal is to change the way you think about sentences, not just tag them.</strong>
633	              </p>
634	              <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.7)]">They won't always be there. If your entire strategy is seek-and-find, you'll be stuck.</p>
635	            </QuoteCard>
636	
637	            <p>
638	              So go deeper. Ask yourself about the personality of each statement. Is it a fact, or is it a claim? Is it
639	              something being offered as evidence, or something being argued for? Facts are premises. Claims are
640	              conclusions.
641	            </p>
642	            <p>
643	              That instinct, reading the role of a sentence rather than hunting for a keyword, is what separates students
644	              who understand arguments from students who just sort of navigate them.
645	            </p>
646	            <p className="font-medium text-[#1a1a1a]">Indicator words will speed you up when they're present. Analytical thinking will save you when they're not.</p>
647	          </div>
648	        </LessonSection>
649	      </>
650	    ),
651	  },
652	  "five-types-of-conclusions": {
653	    eyebrow: "",
654	    title: "Five Types of Conclusions",
655	    subtitle: "Once you find the conclusion, name its type so you can predict the weakness that usually comes with it.",
656	    content: (
657	      <>
658	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Five Types Of Conclusions" className="space-y-8">
659	          <InteractiveCard prominent className="bg-surface p-8 border-none shadow-2">
660	            <h2 className="font-serif text-[36px] font-bold text-ink-bright leading-tight">Five Types of Conclusions</h2>
661	            <p className="mt-4 font-sans text-[15px] text-ink-soft leading-[1.7]">
662	              Once you find the conclusion, name its type so you can predict the weakness that usually comes with it.
663	            </p>
664	          </InteractiveCard>
665	          <div className="space-y-6 font-sans text-[15px] leading-[1.7] text-ink-soft max-w-[64ch]">
666	            <p className="text-ink-bright">The argument did not change. Only the packaging did.</p>
667	            <p>Indicator words are packaging. They can point you toward a conclusion, but they do not tell you what kind of conclusion you are dealing with.</p>
668	            <p className="text-ink-bright font-semibold">That is the real question:</p>
669	            <p className="font-serif text-[28px] italic text-ink-bright leading-[1.3]">What kind of claim is the author making?</p>
670	            <p>
671	              This matters because conclusions are not all built the same way. Each type has its own shape and its own predictable weakness.
672	            </p>
673	            <p className="text-ink-bright font-semibold">
674	              So once you can name the type of conclusion, you are not just "finding the conclusion" anymore. You are already starting to see where the argument might break.
675	            </p>
676	          </div>
677	        </LessonSection>
678	
679	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Recommendations" className="space-y-8">
680	          <div className="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3 gap-6">
681	            <ConclusionTypeCard 
682	              title="Recommendations"
683	              definition="A recommendation tells us what should happen."
684	              example="Double-blind techniques should be used whenever possible in scientific experiments."
685	              weakness="The author has to assume the proposal is better than the costs, risks, or alternatives."
686	              question="Why is that the best move?"
687	              colorClass="hover:border-t-[4px] hover:border-t-[#FACC15]"
688	            />
689	            <ConclusionTypeCard 
690	              title="Causal Hypotheses"
691	              definition="A causal hypothesis claims to know why something happened."
692	              example="Restrictions on tobacco advertisements caused the reduction in the number of people smoking."
693	              weakness="Correlation, timing, or coexistence do not prove cause; something else may explain the result."
694	              question="What else could explain it?"
695	              colorClass="hover:border-t-[4px] hover:border-t-[#A855F7]"
696	            />
697	            <ConclusionTypeCard 
698	              title="Predictions"
699	              definition="A prediction claims that something will happen."
700	              example="Global warming probably will not produce more frequent and intense tropical storms."
701	              weakness="Predictions assume the relevant conditions will stay stable, and often they do not."
702	              question="What could change?"
703	              colorClass="hover:border-t-[4px] hover:border-t-[#38BDF8]"
704	            />
705	            <ConclusionTypeCard 
706	              title="Judgments / Labels"
707	              definition="A judgment assigns a verdict or label."
708	              example="Because the new products are selling poorly, the ad campaign is ill-conceived."
709	              weakness="The label sounds strong, but the evidence may not actually prove that the label fits."
710	              question="Did they actually prove that label fits?"
711	              colorClass="hover:border-t-[4px] hover:border-t-[#10B981]"
712	            />
713	            <ConclusionTypeCard 
714	              title="Rebuttals"
715	              definition="A rebuttal is the author saying someone else is wrong."
716	              example="Critics say this movie is unrealistic. They are wrong, because it is a funny comedy and that is all that matters."
717	              weakness="The author may answer the wrong criticism, reject the other side badly, or never really respond to the point."
718	              question="Did the author actually answer them?"
719	              colorClass="hover:border-t-[4px] hover:border-t-[#F97316]"
720	            />
721	          </div>
722	        </LessonSection>
723	        
724	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Interactive Practice" className="space-y-8">
725	            <ConclusionTypeSwitchboard />
726	        </LessonSection>
727	
728	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Remember" className="space-y-8 border-b-0">
729	          <SectionHeading title="Remember" subtitle="Do not stop at finding the conclusion" />
730	          <div className="grid grid-cols-1 gap-3 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-5">
731	            {[
732	              { type: "Recommendations", note: "say what should happen.", color: "#FACC15" },
733	              { type: "Causal hypotheses", note: "say what caused something.", color: "#A855F7" },
734	              { type: "Predictions", note: "say what will happen.", color: "#38BDF8" },
735	              { type: "Judgments and labels", note: "assign a verdict.", color: "#10B981" },
736	              { type: "Rebuttals", note: "say the other side is wrong.", color: "#F97316" },
737	            ].map((entry) => (
738	              <div
739	                key={entry.type}
740	                className="rounded-[14px] border border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.08)] bg-white px-4 py-4 shadow-[0_10px_24px_rgba(26,26,26,0.04)] transition-transform duration-200 ease-out hover:-translate-y-[2px]"
741	                style={{ borderTopColor: entry.color, borderTopWidth: 3 }}
742	              >
743	                <span className="inline-block h-1.5 w-8 rounded-full" style={{ backgroundColor: entry.color }} />
744	                <h4 className="mt-3 font-serif text-[18px] italic leading-tight text-[#1a1a1a]">{entry.type}</h4>
745	                <p className="mt-1.5 text-[14px] leading-[1.5] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.66)]">{entry.note}</p>
746	              </div>
747	            ))}
748	          </div>
749	          <div className="space-y-4 text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
750	            <p>Do not stop at finding the conclusion. Name the type.</p>
751	            <p>Once you know the type of conclusion, you know the kind of weakness to look for.</p>
752	          </div>
753	        </LessonSection>
754	      </>
755	    ),
756	  },
757	  "beyond-premises-and-conclusions": {
758	    eyebrow: "LESSON",
759	    title: "Beyond Premises and Conclusions",
760	    subtitle: "The mindset shift: separating the argument from context, opposition, concessions, and pivots.",
761	    content: <BeyondPremisesAndConclusions />,
762	  },
763	  "first-sentence-vs-rebuttal-structures": {
764	    eyebrow: "Lessons",
765	    title: "First-Sentence vs Rebuttal Structures",
766	    subtitle: "The main conclusion usually appears in one of two places.",
767	    content: (
768	      <>
769	        <LessonSection tocTitle="Conclusion Placement" className="space-y-16">
770	          <SectionHeading title="Conclusion Placement" subtitle="Where the author usually puts the main point" />
771	          <div className="grid gap-6 lg:grid-cols-2">
772	            <motion.div
773	              initial={{ opacity: 0, y: 18 }}
774	              whileInView={{ opacity: 1, y: 0 }}
775	              viewport={{ once: true, amount: 0.35 }}
776	              transition={{ duration: 0.38, ease: [0.22, 1, 0.36, 1] }}
777	              whileHover={{ y: -3, boxShadow: "0 20px 34px -28px oklch(0.36 0.05 150 / 0.2)" }}
778	              className="group rounded-[24px] border border-[oklch(0.46_0.05_150/0.14)] bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,oklch(0.986_0.006_110),oklch(0.974_0.01_145))] p-7 shadow-[0_18px_38px_-30px_oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.16)] transition-shadow duration-200"
779	            >
780	              <div className="mb-5 flex items-baseline justify-between gap-4">
781	                <span className="text-[10px] font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-[oklch(0.43_0.04_145/0.68)]">Place 1</span>
782	                <span className="rounded-full border border-[oklch(0.46_0.05_150/0.14)] bg-[oklch(0.98_0.01_150/0.86)] px-3 py-1 text-[10px] font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-[oklch(0.39_0.05_145/0.76)] transition-colors duration-200 group-hover:bg-[oklch(0.965_0.018_145)]">
783	                  Up front
784	                </span>
785	              </div>
786	              <h3 className="mb-4 max-w-[13ch] font-serif text-[28px] italic leading-tight text-[oklch(0.16_0.01_60)]">
787	                First-sentence conclusion
788	              </h3>
789	              <div className="space-y-4 text-[18px] leading-[1.68] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
790	                <p>The author states the main point in the first sentence.</p>
791	                <p>The rest of the stimulus gives the support. Main point first. Reasons after.</p>
792	              </div>
793	            </motion.div>
794	            <motion.div
795	              initial={{ opacity: 0, y: 18 }}
796	              whileInView={{ opacity: 1, y: 0 }}
797	              viewport={{ once: true, amount: 0.35 }}
798	              transition={{ duration: 0.42, delay: 0.06, ease: [0.22, 1, 0.36, 1] }}
799	              whileHover={{ y: -3, boxShadow: "0 20px 34px -28px oklch(0.42 0.06 45 / 0.18)" }}
800	              className="group rounded-[24px] border border-[oklch(0.58_0.05_42/0.16)] bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,oklch(0.987_0.006_32),oklch(0.975_0.01_48))] p-7 shadow-[0_18px_38px_-30px_oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.16)] transition-shadow duration-200"
801	            >
802	              <div className="mb-5 flex items-baseline justify-between gap-4">
803	                <span className="text-[10px] font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-[oklch(0.46_0.04_42/0.68)]">Place 2</span>
804	                <span className="rounded-full border border-[oklch(0.58_0.05_42/0.16)] bg-[oklch(0.982_0.01_42/0.86)] px-3 py-1 text-[10px] font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-[oklch(0.43_0.05_40/0.76)] transition-colors duration-200 group-hover:bg-[oklch(0.968_0.02_42)]">
805	                  After the turn
806	                </span>
807	              </div>
808	              <h3 className="mb-4 max-w-[14ch] font-serif text-[28px] italic leading-tight text-[oklch(0.16_0.01_60)]">
809	                Rebuttal / pivot conclusion
810	              </h3>
811	              <div className="space-y-4 text-[18px] leading-[1.68] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
812	                <p>The stimulus starts with someone else&apos;s view, or with language that is not yet the author&apos;s point.</p>
813	                <p>Then the author turns into the real conclusion. Wait for the author&apos;s turn.</p>
814	              </div>
815	            </motion.div>
816	          </div>
817	        </LessonSection>
818	
819	        <LessonSection tocTitle="FOUR ARGUMENT ROLES" className="space-y-6">
820	          <ClaimBucketInspector />

949	export function IntroLessonPage({ pageId }: { pageId: IntroLessonPageId }) {
950	  const page = pages[pageId];
951	
952	  return (
953	    <IntroNotesShell eyebrow={page.eyebrow} title={page.title} subtitle={page.subtitle}>
954	      {page.content}
955	    </IntroNotesShell>
956	  );
957	}

1	import { conditionalUnits, findConditionalUnit } from './content';
2	import { DIFFICULTIES, type ConditionalDifficulty } from './content/types';
3	import { getCurriculumDifficulty, getCurriculumUnits } from './curriculumPath';
4	import { CONDITIONAL_AREAS, DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET, conditionalAssignmentDisplayCopy, resolveConditionalAssignment, type ConditionalAssignmentTarget } from '@/lib/conditionalBootcampAssignment';
5	import { classroomItemMatchesBootcamp } from '@/lib/bootcamps';
6	
7	export interface ConditionalAssignmentRow {
8	  id: string; student_id: string; type: string; title: string; description?: string | null;
9	  source_id: string | null; metadata_json: Record<string, unknown> | null;
10	  status: string; visible_to_student: boolean; unlock_at: string | null;
11	}
12	export interface ResolvedConditionalAssignment {
13	  id: string; title: string; description?: string; route: string; target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget;
14	  unitIds: string[]; mode: 'learn' | 'practice'; difficulty?: ConditionalDifficulty;
15	}
16	function parseTarget(value: unknown): ConditionalAssignmentTarget | null {
17	  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object' || Array.isArray(value)) return null;
18	  const target = value as ConditionalAssignmentTarget;
19	  if (!['course', 'stage', 'area', 'skill', 'general'].includes(target.scope) ||
20	    !['learn', 'practice'].includes(target.mode) || !DIFFICULTIES.includes(target.difficulty) ||
21	    (target.curriculumVersion !== undefined && target.curriculumVersion !== 2)) return null;
22	  if ((target.scope === 'skill' || target.scope === 'stage') && !findConditionalUnit(target.unitId)) return null;
23	  if (target.scope === 'area' && !CONDITIONAL_AREAS.some(area => area.id === target.area)) return null;
24	  return target;
25	}
26	function legacyTarget(source: string | null): ConditionalAssignmentTarget | null {
27	  if (!source || !source.startsWith('/bootcamp/conditionals')) return null;
28	  const url = new URL(source, 'https://local.invalid');
29	  const base = '/bootcamp/conditionals';
30	  if (url.pathname !== base && !url.pathname.startsWith(`${base}/`)) return null;
31	  const level = url.searchParams.get('level') ?? 'beginner';
32	  if (!DIFFICULTIES.includes(level as ConditionalDifficulty)) return null;
33	  const difficulty = level as ConditionalDifficulty;
34	  const start = url.searchParams.get('start');
35	  if (start) return parseTarget({ scope: 'stage', mode: 'learn', difficulty, unitId: start });
36	  const pathUnit = url.pathname.match(/^\/bootcamp\/conditionals\/(?:lessons|activities)\/([^/]+)$/)?.[1];
37	  const unitId = pathUnit ?? url.searchParams.get('unit');
38	  const area = url.searchParams.get('area');
39	  const mode = url.pathname === `${base}/practice` ? 'practice' : 'learn';
40	  if (unitId) return parseTarget({ scope: 'skill', mode, difficulty, unitId });
41	  if (area) return parseTarget({ scope: 'area', mode, difficulty, area });
42	  if (url.pathname === `${base}/practice`) return parseTarget({ scope: 'general', mode: 'practice', difficulty });
43	  return url.pathname === base && !url.search ? { ...DEFAULT_CONDITIONAL_TARGET } : null;
44	}
45	export function conditionalAssignmentUnitIds(target: ConditionalAssignmentTarget): string[] {
46	  if (target.scope === 'course') return getCurriculumUnits(conditionalUnits).map(unit => unit.id);
47	  if (target.scope === 'stage') {
48	    if (target.curriculumVersion === 2) return getCurriculumUnits(conditionalUnits, target.unitId).map(unit => unit.id);
49	    // Existing stage grants refer to the original membership, not a mutable display order.
50	    const originalOpening = ['orientation', 'rules-facts', 'sufficient-necessary', 'truth-validity', 'negation', 'contraposition', 'group-1', 'group-2'];
51	    const originalIds = [...originalOpening, ...conditionalUnits.map(unit => unit.id).filter(id => !originalOpening.includes(id))];
52	    const start = originalIds.indexOf(target.unitId);
53	    return originalIds.slice(Math.max(0, start));
54	  }
55	  return conditionalUnits.filter(unit => target.scope === 'skill' ? unit.id === target.unitId
56	    : target.scope === 'area' ? unit.area === target.area : unit.phase === 'conditional').map(unit => unit.id);
57	}
58	export async function resolveConditionalAssignments(rows: ConditionalAssignmentRow[], userId: string, now = Date.now()): Promise<ResolvedConditionalAssignment[]> {
59	  const result: ResolvedConditionalAssignment[] = [];
60	  for (const row of rows) {
61	    if (row.student_id !== userId || row.type !== 'bootcamp_module' || !row.visible_to_student ||
62	      !['assigned', 'in_progress', 'completed'].includes(row.status) || !classroomItemMatchesBootcamp(row, 'conditionals')) continue;
63	    if (row.unlock_at && (!Number.isFinite(Date.parse(row.unlock_at)) || Date.parse(row.unlock_at) > now)) continue;
64	    const metadata = row.metadata_json;
65	    const target = metadata && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(metadata, 'conditional_target')
66	      ? parseTarget(metadata.conditional_target) : legacyTarget(row.source_id);
67	    if (!target) continue;
68	    const resolved = await resolveConditionalAssignment(target);
69	    result.push({ id: row.id, ...conditionalAssignmentDisplayCopy(resolved.target, resolved, row), route: resolved.route, target: resolved.target,
70	      unitIds: conditionalAssignmentUnitIds(resolved.target), mode: resolved.target.mode,
71	      ...(target.scope !== 'course' && target.scope !== 'stage' ? { difficulty: resolved.target.difficulty } : {}) });
72	  }
73	  return result;
74	}
75	/** URL parameters never grant work. Call this against a target read from the student's assignment row. */
76	export function conditionalAssignmentAllowsAction(assignment: ResolvedConditionalAssignment, request: {
77	  unitId: string; action: 'lesson' | 'guided' | 'practice'; difficulty?: ConditionalDifficulty;
78	}): boolean {
79	  if (!assignment.unitIds.includes(request.unitId)) return false;
80	  if (request.action !== 'practice') return assignment.mode === 'learn';
81	  return request.difficulty === (assignment.difficulty ?? getCurriculumDifficulty(request.unitId));
82	}
83	export async function conditionalTeacherPreview(): Promise<ResolvedConditionalAssignment> {
84	  const resolved = await resolveConditionalAssignment();
85	  return { id: 'teacher-preview', title: resolved.title, route: resolved.route, target: resolved.target,
86	    unitIds: conditionalAssignmentUnitIds(resolved.target), mode: 'learn' };
87	}

6	/** Isolated development harness. It does not mount assignment or account persistence. */
7	export default function ConditionalOpeningPreview() {
8	  const [params, setParams] = useSearchParams();
9	  const [reviewed, setReviewed] = useState(false);
10	  const id = params.get('unit') ?? 'orientation';
11	  const ids = ['orientation', 'sufficient-necessary', 'group-1', 'group-2'];
12	  const unit = findConditionalUnit(ids.includes(id) ? id : 'orientation')!;
13	  const next = ids[(ids.indexOf(unit.id) + 1) % ids.length];
14	  return <main style={{ background: '#e9e7dd', minHeight: '100vh', padding: '20px 12px 70px' }}><nav aria-label="Development lesson preview" style={{ maxWidth: 1060, margin: '0 auto 20px', display: 'flex', flexWrap: 'wrap', gap: 12 }}>{ids.map(value => <button key={value} style={{ padding: '10px 15px' }} aria-pressed={value === unit.id} onClick={() => { setParams({ unit: value }); setReviewed(false); }}>{value}</button>)}</nav><ConditionalLessonNote unit={unit} reviewed={reviewed} onComplete={() => setReviewed(true)} continueHref={`/dev/conditional-opening-preview?unit=${next}`} continueLabel="Preview next lesson"/></main>;
15	}
